## 8.1.12 Documents, Manuals and Information to be Carried CAT.GEN.MPA.180

1. The following documents, manuals and information shall be carried on each Ryanair flight, as originals or copies unless otherwise specified:

a. the Aircraft Flight Manual (AFM), or equivalent document(s);\*

b. the original certificate of registration;

c. the original Certificate of Airworthiness (CofA);

d. the noise certificate, including an English translation, where one has been provided by the IAA;

e. a certified true copy of the Air Operator Certificate (AOC);\*

f. the operations specifications relevant to the aircraft type, issued with the AOC;\*

g. the original aircraft radio licence, if applicable;

h. the third party liability insurance certificate(s);\*

i. the journey log for the aircraft;\*

j. the aircraft technical log, in accordance with Annex I (Part-M) to Regulation (EU) No 1321/2014;

k. details of the filed ATS flight plan, contained in the LIDO OFP\*;

l. current and suitable aeronautical charts for the route of the proposed flight and all routes along which it is reasonable to expect that the flight may be diverted (Jeppesen Charts);\*

m. procedures and visual signals information for use by intercepting and intercepted aircraft (Jeppesen Publications (Pubs) &gt; ICAO Rules of the Air – Annex 2);\*

n. information concerning search and rescue services for the area of the intended flight, which shall be easily accessible in the flight crew compartment (Jeppesen Publications (Pubs) &gt; Airway Manual);\*

o. the current parts of the operations manual that are relevant to the duties of the crew members, which shall be easily accessible to the crew members;\*

p. the MEL;\*

q. appropriate notices to airmen (NOTAMs) and Aeronautical Information Service (AIS) briefing documentation\*;

r. appropriate meteorological information;\*

s. mass and balance documentation;\*

Issue: 22, Revision: 0, Date: 01/01/26

RYANAIR

PART A – OPERATIONS MANUAL

t. the operational flight plan\*;
u. notification of Special Categories of Passenger (SCPs) and special loads; and
v. any other documentation that may be pertinent to the flight or is required by the States concerned with the flight.

Note: \* Item so marked may be carried in an electronic format only.

2. Documents, Manuals and Information to be Carried Electronic format: AMC1 CAT.GEN.MPA.180

The documents, manuals and information may be available in a form other than on printed paper, accessibility, usability and reliability shall be assured.

3. Notwithstanding (1), in the case of loss or theft of documents specified in 1(b) to 1 (h), the operation may continue until the flight reaches its destination or a place where replacement documents can be provided.

## 8.1.12.1 Information to be Retained on the Ground CAT.GEN.MPA.185

1. Ryanair ensures that at least for the duration of each flight or series of flights:
   a. information relevant to the flight and appropriate for the type of operation is preserved on the ground;
   b. the information is retained until it has been duplicated at the place at which it will be stored;

2. The information referred to in (1) includes:
   a. a copy of the operational flight plan;
   b. copies of the relevant part(s) of the aircraft technical log;
   c. route-specific NOTAM documentation if specifically edited Ryanair;
   d. mass and balance documentation (or electronic equivalent); and
   e. special loads notification.

3. A copy of the NOTOC shall be transmitted to:
   a. The destination airport;
   b. Flight Operations in Dublin;
   c. A copy shall be returned to Flight Operations via EFL or in the returned Flight Envelope;
   d. The alternate airport in the event of a diversion and retained by the addressees for the duration of the flight. Information to Passengers and other Persons is provided for in the Ground Operations Manual and SEP Manual.

## 8.1.12.2 Documents, Manuals and Information to be Carried Electronic format: AMC1 CAT.GEN.MPA.180

The documents, manuals and information may be available in a form other than on printed paper, accessibility, usability and reliability shall be assured.

## 8.1.12.3 Documents, Manuals and Information to be Carried the Air Operator Certificate

Certified true copies are provided directly by the IAA.

## 8.1.12.4 Procedures and Visual Signals for use by Intercepting and Intercepted Aircraft AMC1 CAT.GEN.MPA.180(a)(13)

The procedures and the visual signals for use by intercepting and intercepted aircraft reflect those contained in the International Civil Aviation Organisation (ICAO) Annex 2. This is part of the operations manual, OMC Jeppesen General Airway Manual.

## 8.1.12.5 Personal Documents and Information to be Carried

Flight Crew:

- Valid Pilots Licence with Type Rating.
- Instrument Ratings.
- R/T Licence (included in ATPL).
- Passport and Visas if required.
- Vaccination certs. if required.
- Current Medical Certificate.

Cabin Crew:

- Passport and visas if required.
- Vaccination certs. if required.
- ATQV or cert of competency or Licence.

## 8.1.12.5.1 Action in the Event of Lost/Stolen Documents – Flight Crew

Should a crew member’s flight documentation be lost or stolen it is permitted for that crew member to operate for a maximum of 1 flight duty period in accordance with the procedure set out below.

- Inform the IAA or relevant aviation authority immediately to organise replacement documents and obtain a time frame for replacement.
- Lost documents shall be considered stolen, inform local police authority.
- Inform crew control of the loss/theft and provide a time frame for replacement.
- Print a copy of pilot licence and medical from Electronic record keeping system.
- The printed documents will include;

1. Licence Parts I – XII.
2. Validity, Radiotelephony Privileges &amp; Remarks.

## 8.2 Ground Handling Instructions

## 8.2.0 General

## 8.2.0.1 On-time Departure – Punctuality

Departures "on-time" is a key measure of the performance of a scheduled airline.

BLOCK TIME is defined as the time between an aeroplane first moving from its parking place for the purpose of taking off until it comes to rest on the designated parking position and all engines are stopped.

The Commander shall state ‘OFF-BLOCKS hh.mm’ as the time movement commences with pushback (or taxi for self-manoeuvring operations) for the purpose of taking off and this shall be recorded on the Electronic Flight Log/Paper Voyage Report.

The Commander shall state ‘ON-BLOCKS hh.mm’ which shall be recorded on the Electronic Flight Log/Paper Voyage Report as the time when the last engine decelerates through 20% N2, (when pilots select the anti-collision light switch OFF). When Crewlink entries are made by the crew these shall be the times recorded on the Electronic Flight Log/Paper VR as stated by the Commander.

ARMD movement times are electronically recorded and uploaded on Crewlink/Crew Management System in the following hierarchy:

1. Crew entries;
2. Aircraft automatic recording;
3. Station SITA message.

From the above hierarchy crews can override ARMD entries. When the crew record is different than the ARMD record this shall be reported via Zendesk.

It is Ryanair policy to depart early when possible and departures ahead of schedule are permitted and should be anticipated. To achieve an on-time departure requires timely completion of a series of aircraft servicing and passenger handling activities. Commanders shall monitor these activities and use their authority to influence and facilitate the process.

page 8–134

Issue: 22, Revision: 0, Date: 01/01/26

RYANAIR

PART A – OPERATIONS MANUAL

Safety is our primary operational objective and crews shall resist any propensity to accelerate or rush the normal delivery of pre-flight/before start activities to avoid a delayed departure time. The integrity of checklist completion must never be compromised.

All components of a delayed departure shall be recorded on the Electronic Flight Log using the IATA Delay Codes.

## 8.2.0.2 Passenger Boarding Without Flight Crew on Board

Boarding without flight crew will only commence when the Cabin Crew have been instructed to do so by Operations Control and is only permitted if the following conditions can be met:

- Flight crew estimated arrival time is expected to be under 20 minutes.
- Fuelling is not taking place.
- Cabin crew have established the outside air temperature to be 25 degrees or less.
- The APU is off.
- Ground power is connected and running.
- Mobile steps must remain in place until at least one flight crew arrives.
- Cabin lights are illuminated.

## Designated GHA Duties

- The ‘Designated GHA’ (DGHA) is identified to the CSS thereby confirming both are aware that this is a ‘boarding without flight crew onboard’ procedure.
- The DGHA shall have a serviceable radio and/or a mobile phone.
- The DGHA remains on station, within 20 m of the aircraft L1 door, in continuous line of sight of the CSS position and ready to act without delay until at least one flight crew arrives on board.
- The DGHA will alert the CSS to any event outside the aircraft which may affect the safety of passengers, crew or the aircraft.
- The DGHA will without delay, summon emergency or airport services required either by the developing situation at the aircraft or as instructed by the CSS for any situation on board the aircraft.

## CSS Duties

- CSS is responsible for aircraft safety until arrival of the Flight Crew and shall have authority to give all commands he/she deems necessary for the purpose of securing the safety of the aircraft and of persons or property carried therein.

Issue: 22, Revision: 0, Date: 01/01/26

RYANAIR

PART A – OPERATIONS MANUAL

All persons carried in the Aircraft shall obey such commands.

- The CSS will maintain the DGHA in line of sight during boarding and when boarding is completed until at least one flight crew member is on board.
- The CSS will without delay, alert and brief the DGHA when there is a situation on board requiring any intervention from services on the ground following the PAA Brief concept.

## PAA

- Problem: State the problem to the DGHA.
- Action: (Stay onboard, disembarkation, rapid disembarkation, evacuation).
- Additional Information: Airport Emergency Services (Fire/Medical/Police etc.) required/not required.

## The CSS shall on arriving at the aircraft:

1. Shall select the Ground Service button to “on” in order to illuminate cabin lights.
2. Inform the Dispatcher or available Ground Crew member that Flight Crew is not present and that the boarding without flight crew procedure will be applied.

## Embarkation

1. The Cabin crew will complete a security search, the CSS will record the time this is completed and inform the Commander when he arrives onboard the aircraft.
2. The CSS will brief the Cabin Crew on the alternative means of communication e.g. Loudhailers if an urgent situation develops as the Cabin Crew interphone will not be available.
3. The CSS will indicate assistance is required by calling or waving both arms, the DGHA goes to the vicinity of the L1 door to receive a briefing (PAA format).

## Arrival of Flight Crew

1. The CSS will commence the boarding PA, this confirms that the flight crew are onboard and completes PDIs on the interphone.
2. The CSS shall confirm with the Commander that all PDIs have been completed and the time at which the security search was completed.
3. The Flight Crew complete the pre-flight briefing.
4. The CSS passes required information to the remaining Cabin Crew before the departure.

RYANAIR

PART A – OPERATIONS MANUAL

5. The CSS alerts the DGHA and confirms that the boarding without flight crew procedure is completed.

Note 1: Crew on Airport Duty may board passengers without Flight Crew. The Airport Duty crew must be from the same AOC as the Aircraft or qualified to operate on the AOC of the aircraft.

Note 2: In all cases, a full crew complement, including a CSS must be present to complete PDIs and Security Searches. The CSS will assign roles to the crew.

Note 3: PDIs and Security Searches may be completed by Airport Duty Crew.

Note 4: Crew on Airport Duty shall confirm with the operating CSS that all PDIs have been completed and the time at which the security search was completed.

Note 5: The operating Crew may take over once all PDIs and Security Search have been completed.

## 8.2.0.3 Operations on the Ground with APU Inoperative and High Outside Air Temperature

The MEL for the B737-800/8200 allows for dispatch with the APU inoperative. Dispatch with the APU – INOP presents additional consideration for engine starting and control of temperature in the cabin.

Good communication and briefing between flight crew, cabin crew and ground crew is essential when an aircraft is required to operate with an APU – INOP. Operations Control Centre will make best efforts to allocate the lines of flying to the most suitable destinations. Ideally APU – INOP aircraft will be allocated to two sector duties limiting the exposure to the number of turnarounds.

The temperature in the cabin can rise very quickly with passengers on board even in comparatively mild temperatures. There have been several significant disruptive passenger incidents caused by rising cabin temperatures that could have been avoided with proactive management, good communications and forward planning.

Handling agents will be notified in advance by OCC that the arriving aircrafts APU is INOP, however crews should call ahead and ensure the following are available:

- Ground power ready
- ASU ready
- Ground source air-conditioning

Issue: 22, Revision: 0, Date: 01/01/26

RYANAIR

PART A – OPERATIONS MANUAL

The following restrictions apply to an aircraft with an inoperative APU:

- The Commander shall not depart to a destination CO-NOTAM’d as having “No ASU”.
- If departing to a destination where the temperature is expected to be higher than 30 degrees centigrade then the commander shall contact OCC to confirm that ground source conditioned air is available.

## Arrival

Taxing in, crews should take every opportunity to cool the cabin as much as possible, flight crew should ensure that all cabin temperature selectors are selected to maximize cooling – C. In the case of direct sunlight entering the cabin, the Commander should instruct cabin crew to lower window blinds on the effected side after disembarkation when completing the security checks.

## On Stand

Commanders should request that ground source air conditioning where available be connected as soon as possible when ambient temperatures are above 25 degrees centigrade. If fixed base air-conditioning is not available, then a mobile unit should be requested. Crews should note that if an air-conditioning unit is not available then certain air start have the ability to be used for cabin air conditioning, GOPs will advise.

## Boarding

Maximize the use of the ground sources air-conditioning. In ambient temperatures above 25 degrees ensure that air conditioning is switched on prior to boarding. Commanders should ensure that cabin temperature is maintained by reference to the forward/aft cabin temperature selector and good communications with the No.1.

If no ground source air-conditioning is available and ambient temperatures are above 25 degrees centigrade, good communications is required to manage the cabin temperature.

Pre-boarding Commanders shall:

- Brief No1 that all cabin doors are to remain open until they are advised to close, this should be advised once start clearance has been received.
- Brief the GHA and confirm ASU serviceable connected and ready to start.
- Brief No1 any delay to start clearance such as tech ASU, missed slot etc will require cancel start clearance and reopen all cabin doors.

page 8–138

Issue: 22, Revision: 0, Date: 01/01/26

RYANAIR

PART A – OPERATIONS MANUAL

Commanders shall ensure that the No1 engine is started at the earliest opportunity to provide air-conditioning once boarding is complete and doors are closed. If extended delays are expected, crews should push, start both engines and Taxi out/hold remotely. Consideration to extra fuel burn during fuel uplift planning is advised. If remote holding is not available boarding should be delayed coordinating completion of boarding in accordance with CTOT.

## Failure of APU during Turnaround

In the event of an APU becoming inoperative after boarding is completed, and where air-conditioning cannot be provided, if the cabin temperatures rises above 25 degrees centigrade the commander shall make all the necessary GOPS arrangements for all passengers to be disembarked.

The Commander should review options with the OCC and Engineering before making the decision when to re-board the passengers.

## Failure of APU during Push Back and Engine Start

Following an APU failure during start and a return to stand, ensure that ground source air-conditioning is connected as a priority, if ground source air-conditioning ASU is not immediately available and the ambient temperatures are approaching 25 degrees centigrade then the Commander should ensure all cabin doors are opened and make the necessary GOPS arrangements for disembarkation of passengers into the terminal until an air start unit is available.

## On Stand Delays with APU Available

When on stand with passengers boarded and doors closed cabin crew should be briefed to closely monitor cabin temperatures especially in the middle of the cabin and to report any evidence of cabin overheating to the captain at the first indication of passenger unrest.

## 8.2.1 Special Refuelling or Defuelling of the Aircraft

CAT.OP.MPA.200

Ryanair has performed a risk assessment, developed procedures and has established a training programme for its personnel involved in Special Refuelling or Defuelling of the aircraft.

Special refuelling or defuelling applies to refuelling or defuelling with passengers embarking, on board, or disembarking.

Any special refuelling or defuelling procedures and any change to them shall require prior approval by the IAA.

PART A – OPERATIONS MANUAL

## 8.2.1.1 Refuelling, Ground Equipment, Electrical Switching, Defueling Procedures and Precautions

The refuelling procedures in this manual are shared with Engineering and GOPS in their relevant manuals to the extent that Engineering and Ground OPS personnel are aware of refuelling procedures and in particular the designation of duties of the Refuelling Supervisor. Ryanair aircraft may be fuelled with an APU running, and/or GPU/ASU or other ground equipment attached. Electrical switching is permitted, but HF, when fitted, shall not be used. Refuelling or defueling is not authorised with engine(s) running.

Ryanair aircraft are not normally defueled. Consult Maintrol if defueling is a possibility.

Ryanair aircraft shall not be refueled with wide cut fuel.

Ryanair contracts with refuelling service provider should ensure that wide-cut fuel cannot be supplied to Ryanair aircraft.

If the commander suspects that wide cut fuel has been supplied he shall contact maintrol immediately. Flights can continue but Maintrol will supply special procedures to be applied and the NPFO shall authorise the flight or series of flights until it has been established that there is no more wide-cut fuel in the fuel system.

## 8.2.1.2 Refuelling or Defueling with Passengers Embarking/On Board/Disembarking

CAT.OP.MPA.200(b)(2)

Definitions to be used in this section.

**Qualified Person** – A person on board the aircraft for refuelling operations who is capable of handling emergency procedures concerning fire protection and firefighting, handling communications, and initiating and directing an evacuation.

**Refuelling Supervisor**: A member of ground crew and/or air crew supervising the refuelling operation. A Refuelling Supervisor will alert the Qualified Person without delay if any hazardous situation occurs.

Refuelling supervision can be completed by:

1. Multiple persons tasked with specific functional roles (distributed supervision) during special refuelling operations (RFS-A).
2. A single person assigned by the commander (RFS-B).

## Refuelling or Defuelling with Passengers Embarking, on Board or Disembarking

AMCS CAT.OP.MPA.200

When refuelling or defueling with passengers embarking, on board, or disembarking, the necessary precautions should be taken, and the aircraft should be properly manned by qualified personnel that should be ready to initiate and direct an evacuation of the aircraft by the most practical and expeditious means available.

## Operational Procedures with Passengers Embarking, on Board or Disembarking

ALT/24/2011

1. When refuelling/defuelling with passengers on board, ground servicing activities and work inside the aeroplane, such as catering and cleaning, should be conducted in such a manner that they do not create a hazard and allow emergency evacuation through those aisles and exits that are intended for emergency evacuation.

2. The deployment of integral aeroplane stairs or the opening of emergency exits are not necessarily a prerequisite to refuelling.

3. Operational procedures specify that at least the following precautions are taken:

a. one qualified person should remain at a specified location during refuelling/defuelling operations with passengers on board, and be capable of using emergency procedures for fire protection and firefighting, communications, as well as for initiating and directing an evacuation;

b. two-way communication should be established and remain available through suitable means, between the ground crew that supervises the refuelling and the qualified personnel on board the aeroplane;

c. crew, personnel, and passengers should be warned that refuelling/defuelling will take place;

d. the ‘FASTEN SEAT BELT’ signs should be off;

e. ‘NO SMOKING’ signs should be on, together with interior lighting to allow the identification of emergency exits;

f. passengers should be instructed to unfasten their seat belts and refrain from smoking;

g. the minimum required number of cabin crew should be on board and prepared for an immediate emergency evacuation;

h. if fuel vapour is detected inside the aeroplane, or any other hazard arises, refuelling/defuelling should be stopped immediately;
i. the ground area beneath the exits that are intended for emergency evacuation, as well as slide deployment areas, should be kept clear where stairs are not in position for use in the event of evacuation; and
j. provision is made for a safe and rapid evacuation.

## 8.2.1.2.1

Refuelling is permitted with passengers embarking, on board or disembarking. Refuelling of aircraft is a routine procedure and precautions are always taken to preclude the possibility of fire, fuel spillage, etc. however, when passengers are involved, additional precautions must be taken to ensure that they can be disembarked/evacuated in the unlikely event of a hazard arising that affects safety.

Refuelling with passengers embarking, on board or disembarking is subject to the approval of the local Airport Authority and may not be available at certain airports.

## 8.2.1.2.2 Roles and Responsibilities

### 8.2.1.2.2.1 Flight Crew Responsibilities

The Commander designates a Qualified Person on board the aircraft for refuelling operations with passengers on board, embarking or disembarking who:

- Is capable of handling emergency procedures concerning fire protection and firefighting, handling communications, and initiating and directing an evacuation.
- Is a Ryanair Group pilot qualified on type.
- Shall remain on the flight deck.
- May also be a Refuelling Supervisor.
- The Commander shall determine the refuelling supervision based on whether RFS-A, or RFS-B applies.

The Commander is responsible for ensuring that the refuelling procedures are followed during the refuelling operation and shall ensure the following:

- The applicable refuelling procedure is confirmed from the RFS approved airport list prior to commencement of refuelling.
- The applicable refuelling procedure is announced to the cabin crew.
- The aircraft is suitably immobilised by means of brakes and/or chocks.

- Passengers and Cabin Crew are informed so that Cabin Crew are appropriately positioned for emergency egress procedures.
- Interphone is serviceable between flight deck and cabin, or alternatively, and by prior arrangement, the qualified person on the flight deck briefs the SCCM to be in the forward galley to receive any instructions from the flight deck.
- The SCCM is advised of any problems outside the aircraft which might affect the Cabin Crews’ decision on door usage in the event of an evacuation being required.
- Where a passenger boarding bridge is not in use, steps shall be in place at the doors (L1 or L2).
- The “No Smoking” sign shall be switched on and the “Seat Belt” sign shall be switched off.
- If advised that fuel vapour is detected within the cabin, fuelling must be ceased until it is safe to resume.
- Remain vigilant for fire or other warning of an unsafe condition via headset or visual communication and be prepared to initiate passenger evacuation if necessary.
- If a hazard arises, communicate with the refuelling operator to ensure that fuelling shall be stopped immediately until conditions permit resumption.
- Before and during refuelling, where applicable and in consultation with the GHA, ensure that Fire Service facilities are available.
- Every case of fuel spillage, irrespective of the amount, shall be reported immediately to the Airport Authorities. In this case, the flight crew member in the cockpit shall notify ATC that a fuel spillage has occurred and shall include details of the aircraft parking position.
- The commander shall ensure that, prior to the commencement of special refuelling, a suitable means to communicate with the qualified personnel on board the aircraft is available.

Note: When there are no passengers embarking, on board or disembarking the qualified person does not have to remain in the flight deck.

## 8.2.1.2.2.2 Refuelling Supervisor Responsibilities (RFS A)

The primary responsibility of a Refuelling Supervisor is to promptly alert the qualified person on board to any emerging hazard.

During refuelling with passengers embarking, on board, or disembarking, ‘the Qualified Person’ as designated by the commander, shall remain on the flight deck in a suitable position to maintain visual contact with the fuelling environment.

Issue: 22, Revision: 0, Date: 01/01/26

RYANAIR

PART A – OPERATIONS MANUAL

In order to ensure appropriate refuelling supervision during special refuelling operations, the Commander must:

- Announce ‘RFS A’ to the Cabin Crew.
- Advise the GHA that special refuelling will take place. The GHA will inform the ground staff team to be alert to any fuelling hazard that may require them to apply their respective procedures.
- Advise the Refuelling Operator as to when refuelling can commence.
- With both flight crew in the flight deck, each flight crew member supervises his respective area of the refuelling zone.
- Anytime there is only one flight crew member in the flight deck, he shall occupy the right-hand seat.
- During refuelling supervision crews can complete other cockpit duties, however, these duties must not detract from the overall supervision of refuelling operations.

During the refuelling process, the Fuelling Operator is viewable from the right-hand seat in the flight deck. In the case of any hazard or incident that may affect the safe conduct of refuelling operations, the following procedure shall apply to ensure that the fuelling stops immediately:

The Flight Crew shall:

- Visually attract the attention of the Refuelling Operator and/or use the ‘GRD Call’ pushbutton; or
- Call on the interphone system if the Refuelling Operator is using a headset; or
- Open the flight deck side window and use direct verbal or non-verbal communication including the use of hand signals; or,
- Dispatch the other flight crew member, or any other ground personnel to the Refuelling Operator.

The Refuelling Operator shall:

- Ensure that the fuelling stops immediately (prepare to move the bowser away from the aircraft if necessary).
- Attract the attention of the Flight Crew by any means available, including:
- Banging on fuselage at flight deck position,
- Call on the interphone system if using a headset,
- Visual signals,

PART A – OPERATIONS MANUAL

- Using ‘PILOT CALL’ pushbutton,
- Verbal communication via open side window,
- Entering aircraft to notify crew directly,
- Alert and dispatch any ground personnel in the vicinity to help.

In the event that two-way visual or audio communication becomes unavailable, or anytime supervision of the refuelling environment is impaired (e.g., poor apron lighting, inclement weather such as heavy rain/snow, or a ground handling vehicle blocking line-of-sight, etc.) refuelling shall be suspended until:

- Two-way visual or audio communication is re-established, or
- RFS Procedure B procedures below are commenced.

Note: The Qualified Person may also be a Refuelling Supervisor.

## 8.2.1.2.2.2.1 Refuelling Supervisor Responsibilities (RFS B)

Note: RFS B is applied where local by-laws do not allow the use of RFS-A.

The primary responsibility of the Refuelling Supervisor is to promptly alert the qualified person on board to any emerging hazard.

- Observe the safety of the Refuelling Operator,
- Verify that the refuelling operation remains safe,
- Maintain a suitable means to communicate with the qualified person,
- Ensure correct and prompt action is taken in response to any emerging emergency situation.

The Refuelling Supervisor shall:

- Position himself externally with access to the interphone connection point adjacent to the nose wheel, whereby communications are established between the Qualified Person and the Refuelling Supervisor using the aircraft intercommunications system and maintain the refuelling operator in sight during refuelling.
- Be trained in the Ryanair Aircraft refuelling procedures.
- Be trained in the operation of the aircraft communication systems.

The Refuelling Supervisor can complete other tasks (e.g., completing the walkaround) providing he can see the Refuelling Operator and remains within easy reach of a system of communication.

Note: Local by-laws may require the Refuelling Supervisor to remain on the headset at all times. (Refer to the ‘Refuelling Supervision Airport List’ on EFB.)

## 8.2.1.2.2.3 Refuelling Operator Responsibilities

Refuelling Operators using own headsets have received own company training accepted by Ryanair to operate the refuelling panel and to establish communications using the aircraft communications system with the Qualified Person on the flight deck.

The Refuelling Operator shall:

- Ensure that the aircraft is bonded to the refuelling tanker/fuel pit.
- Establish visual communication with the Flight Crew.
- Ensure that refuelling does not commence without prior authorization from the flight crew or other person designated by the commander.
- Ensure that when hazards, unsafe acts or incidents related to refuelling operations are identified, stop the refuelling immediately.
- Be responsible for the safe positioning of the fuel truck, awareness/maintenance of the vehicle escape path and awareness to keep clear emergency evacuation paths from the aircraft.
- Notify the Qualified Person as soon as possible when hazards, unsafe acts or incidents in his area are identified.

## 8.2.1.2.2.4 Cabin Crew Special Refuelling Supervisory Responsibilities

As part of their responsibilities Cabin Crew are tasked to promptly alert the qualified person on board to any emerging hazard that they may identify, inside or outside of the aircraft. The SCCM must inform the Commander if there are any PRMs on board whose presence could affect refuelling procedures and ensure that SCPs are not seated in direct access seating and their embarkation or disembarkation does not block exits intended for emergency exit.

The SCCM shall inform all cabin crew that fuelling is about to take place.

- Cabin Crew shall be at their designated location.
- Cabin Crew shall make a PA announcement, advising passengers that fuelling is taking place, that their seatbelts must be unfastened.
- Cabin Crew shall immediately inform the flight crew of:
- Any problems within the cabin during boarding and fuelling.
- Passengers smoking.

- Abnormal build-up of fumes.
- Aisles/exits blocked or congested.
- Any abnormality that increases risk.

- Additionally, during special refuelling (RFS A).
- Cabin Crew assigned to the L1 and L2 doors must remain particularly vigilant for any hazard outside (including, but not limited to, passengers smoking, passengers walking under the wing, fuel leaking from the wing vent, etc.).

## 8.2.1.2.2.5 Ground Crew Special Refuelling Supervisory Responsibilities

As part of their responsibilities Ground Crew are tasked to promptly alert the qualified person on board to any emerging hazard that they may identify, inside or outside the aircraft.

- Ensuring that a clear path is maintained from the aircraft to allow for the quick removal of fuelling vehicles, if required. (Ramp/Fuellers).
- Ensuring that only one aircraft door may be accessed by a service vehicle (e.g., catering/cleaning truck). Passenger steps/Airbridges are deemed to be emergency exit paths and are not considered to be service vehicles. An Ambulift in position to embark/disembark PRMs is considered an emergency exit path.
- Ensuring that the ground area beneath aircraft exit doors not in use is kept clear of obstruction and that a safe and rapid evacuation is possible.
- In the unlikely event that the Fuelling Operator has become incapacitated the Ground Crew shall notify the Flight Crew and assist as required.
- When marshalling passengers, remain vigilant that passengers do not stray into the refuelling safety zone.
- When marshalling passengers who are embarking or disembarking during special refuelling operations, remain in the assigned position and remain vigilant for any fuel leaking from any wing vent.

## 8.2.1.2.2.6 Suitable Means of Communication During Special Refuelling Operations

The following methods of communications are permitted for use when refuelling with Passengers embarking, on board or disembarking:

- Use of the aircraft intercommunications system
- Use of the GRD CALL push button
- Use of the PILOT CALL push button
- Direct verbal/non-verbal communication

- Use of Hand Signals and or banging on the aircraft fuselage
- Entering/exiting the aircraft to communicate directly
- Dispatching a member of the crew to alert the Refuelling Operator

Any of these methods can be used by the refuelling supervisors, qualified personnel, and the refuelling operator provided that the method used is the most effective and expedient manner to relay information related to a developing hazard during special refuelling operations.

## 8.2.1.2.2.7 Fuel Operator Service Levels

The Refuelling Operator carries out the aircraft refuelling including making necessary nozzle connections to the fuel hydrant pit and the aircraft, operating the fuel vehicle and operating the deadman control. The Refuelling Operator opens, closes and operates the switching in the refuelling panel. When the Refuelling Operator does not operate the refuelling panel this is done by an Engineer or one of the Flight Crew.

**Note:** The fuel bowser may be connected to the aircraft immediately on arrival on stand after engines are shut down and the anti-collision light is switched OFF but refuelling shall only be commenced with passengers embarking, on board, or disembarking, on the direction of the Commander or his designate.

## 8.2.1.2.2.8 Reporting – Special Refuelling Procedures

As a minimum, an Air Safety Report must contain the following details in respect of a fuel leak or spillage:

- Refuelling Procedure in force (A or B)
- The location of the leak/spillage
- The means of identification of the emerging/actual hazard
- Method of communication to the Qualified Person and the Refuelling Operator
- Any specific precautions taken (including precautionary disembarkation)
- Any breakdown in communication between the Qualified Person/Refuelling Supervisors/Refuelling Operator/Airport Services

## 8.2.1.2.2.9 On Completion of Refuelling

On completion of refuelling, the Qualified Person shall ensure that:

- The Cabin Crew are informed that refuelling is completed.
- The “Seat Belt” sign is switched ON.

## Line Operations Safety Procedures During Refuelling

1. Consider not commencing refuelling if brakes or engines show signs of being over-heated.

2. Ensure no HF transmissions.
3. Ensure no mobile phone usage within 3.5 m of a refuelling point (i.e. under the right wing).
4. Ryanair flights shall not be fuelled in a hangar.

## 8.2.1.3 Uplift Check

The Commander shall **always** perform an Uplift Check using the fuel record figures in the Technical Log after every refuelling to provide assurance of the fuel quantity system accuracy.

The Uplift Check consists of comparing the sum of the arrival fuel plus the uplift fuel with the total of the gauge readings after refuelling.

The tolerance is ±200 kg for the Boeing 737 aircraft. If refuellers supply in excess of the fuel requested the Commander shall annotate the circumstances on the Electronic flight log/Paper Voyage Report/Journey Log and ideally on the fuel docket. In this case attach the fuel docket to the EFL or return the flight.

If the Commander has any reason to doubt the integrity of the fuel quantity on board or the FQIS, then the drip sticks must be used to confirm the quantity of fuel in the tanks.

The acceptable tolerance between any tank gauge reading and the drip stick figure is ±200 kg for the Boeing 737. Such a large figure is only acceptable due to the difficulty of reading the drip sticks and making the corrections for pitch and roll attitudes. Differences approaching this magnitude will be subject to further uplift checks on subsequent refuellings.

In such circumstances, Commanders shall refer to MEL and consider taking additional fuel in excess of flight plan fuel for the sector.

## 8.2.2 Passenger and Cargo Handling Procedures Related to Safety

### CAT.OP.MPA.165 Passenger Seating

Ryanair has established procedures to ensure that passengers are seated where, in the event that an emergency evacuation is required, they are able to assist and not hinder evacuation of the aircraft.

Compliance with OMB – Mass and Balance assures that no unsafe loading condition arises during embarking and disembarking, loading or unloading.

Boeing anti tipping procedures are stated in the GOPS manual and SEP Manual, and are particularly relevant when disembarking via an airbridge/jetway.

page 8–150

Issue: 22, Revision: 0, Date: 01/01/26

RYANAIR

PART A – OPERATIONS MANUAL

It is essential that all crew are observant of passenger movement and distribution in the cabin during embarkation and disembarkation to prevent an out-of-balance condition which could lead to an aircraft tipping event. The crewmember/GHA performing the exterior inspection and/or refuelling supervision shall immediately contact the flight deck and ground crew should they observe excessive nose-gear strut extension, tipping, or abnormal off/loading or loading procedures. If required, the PIC/CSS should halt embarkation or disembarkation until the condition causing the tipping risk has been resolved.

## Standard/Adjusted Airbridge Boarding

Adjusted Airbridge Boarding is NOT PERMITTED when Engineering Spares (AOG)/Cargo/Hold baggage are scheduled to be loaded or unloaded in aft holds.

Ryanair utilizes two styles of airbridge boarding – standard and adjusted airbridge boarding:

- Standard boarding (2 queues – Priority/Other Q).
- Adjusted Airbridge Boarding (3 queues – Priority/Seat Row 22–33 (B737-800) or 23–35 (B737-8200)/All other customers).

“Adjusted Airbridge Boarding” applies after the dispatcher confirms:

- Holds 3 and 4 empty of Engineering Spares/Cargo.
- Engineering Spares/Cargo will not be loaded into Holds 3 or 4 during the boarding process.
- Bags will only be loaded in Hold 3 AFTER loading of Hold 2 is complete and only towards end of boarding.
- Prior to embarkation, dispatcher informs the Captain “Adjusted Airbridge Boarding Applies.”

The PIC must always confirm with the CSS prior to airbridge embarkation which boarding style applies: “standard airbridge boarding” or “adjusted airbridge boarding.” The CSS will then advise the cabin crew prior to embarkation.

When “Standard Airbridge Boarding” applies, anticipate the following sequence:

- Priority passengers board first.
- All other passengers.

When “Adjusted Airbridge Boarding” applies, anticipate the following sequence:

- Priority passengers board first.
- Passengers seated in Rows 22–33 (B737-800) or 23–35 (B737-8200).

Issue: 22, Revision: 0, Date: 01/01/26

RYANAIR

PART A – OPERATIONS MANUAL

- All other passengers.

Ryanair does not normally carry third party cargo. Cargo on Ryanair aircraft is Engineering Stores or other company mail. It is loaded in accordance with Operations Manual Part B – Mass and Balance.

## 8.2.2.1 Carriage of Special Categories of Passengers (SCPs) CAT.OP.MPA.155

1. Persons requiring special conditions, assistance and/or devices when carried on a flight shall be considered as SCPs including at least:

a. persons with reduced mobility (PRMs) who, without prejudice to Regulation (EC) No 1107/2006, are understood to be any person whose mobility is reduced due to any physical disability, sensory or locomotory, permanent or temporary, intellectual disability or impairment, any other cause of disability, or age;

b. infants and unaccompanied children; and

c. deportees, inadmissible passengers or prisoners in custody.

2. The number of SCPs that may be carried on a given flight is specified in the SEP Manual

3. SCPs shall not be allocated, nor occupy, seats that permit direct access to emergency exits or where their presence could:

a. impede crew members in their duties;

b. obstruct access to emergency equipment; or

c. impede the emergency evacuation of the aircraft.

4. The commander shall be notified in advance when SCPs are to be carried on board.

A passenger who books WCHR assistance for convenience rather than necessity is not considered an SCP.

For the purpose of passenger classification: (Annex I definitions).

1. ‘adult’ means a person of an age of 12 years and above;

2. ‘child/children’ means persons who are of an age of two years and above but who are less than 12 years of age;

3. ‘infant’ means a person under the age of two years.

## 8.2.2.1.1 Travelling with an Accompanying Person (ABP)

Where a passenger is not self-reliant, Ryanair requires that they must travel with an able-bodied accompanying person aged 16 and over. Ryanair has adopted the guidelines as set out by the UK Department for Transport – Access to Air Travel for Disabled People: Code of Practice (July 2008). Walk on/walk off passengers travelling with an ABP are not included in the PRM figures.

## 8.2.2.1.2 Guidelines

If a passenger is not self-reliant in accordance with OMA SEP 2.17.1, then he or she must travel with an able-bodied accompanying person aged 16 and over who will be capable of providing the assistance required.

## 8.2.2.1.3 Seat Allocation for Disabled/Reduced Mobility Passengers

CAT.OP.MPA.165, SEP 5.4.5

The Ryanair computerised allocated seating booking programme should, and Ryanair cabin crew will ensure that access to emergency equipment and evacuation of the aircraft in an emergency will not be impeded before allocating seats.

The following categories may not be allocated direct access seating:

1. Passengers with reduced mobility, which may include but not be limited to someone with a mobility or sensory impairment.
2. Passengers whose physical size would prevent them from being able to move quickly.

The final decision regarding seat allocation in the cabin is made by the SCCM.

## 8.2.2.1.4 Blind or Significant Vision Impaired Passengers (BSVIP)

Blind or Significant Vision Impaired Passengers who meet all of the self-reliant requirements (OMA SEP 2.17.1) may travel without an accompanying person.

A Blind or Significant Vision Impaired Passenger who requires assistance to board or disembark, or to move around the cabin (e.g., assisted to their seat), regardless if travelling alone or accompanied by an ABP, is subject to Safety Limit of 4. Otherwise, Safety Limit of 50% of ABP’s applies.

Travel with an assistance dog is permitted on certain routes (refer to OMA 8.2.2.1.6). Blind or Significant Vision Impaired Passenger travelling with a guide or assistance dog are always subject to a Safety Limit of 4.

Issue: 22, Revision: 0, Date: 01/01/26

RYANAIR

PART A – OPERATIONS MANUAL

## 8.2.2.1.5 Deaf or Significant Hearing Impaired Passengers (DSHIP)

Deaf or Significant Hearing Impaired passengers are not subject to SCP Safety Limit of 4, unless travelling with a guide dog. They are, however, subject to seating restrictions.

Deaf passengers who are able to fully understand an onboard safety demonstration and leaflet are not required to notify Ryanair of their condition.

Where a deaf passenger requires any individual assistance, they are required to indicate this in their booking or contact the Ryanair Special Assistance team.

## Hard of Hearing Passengers

If a passenger is hard of hearing but uses a corrective device which enables the passenger to hear clearly, they are not considered an SCP.

## 8.2.2.1.6 Travel of Assistance/Guide Dogs

Ryanair may carry a maximum of four Assistance or Guide dogs in total on any one flight. The passenger responsible for the assistance/guide dog must be in possession of documentation which confirms that the dog has been trained to perform a specific function.

Ryanair Check-in staff or Ground Handling Agents confirm the necessary documentation has been checked in order for the animal to travel. Dogs must have a pet passport or, in countries where pet passports are not issued, they must have an official veterinary health certificate.

Ryanair Cabin Crew are only required to confirm that the assistance dog is listed on the boarding card.

## 8.2.2.1.7 Battery Operated Wheelchairs

Wet cell battery operated wheelchairs cannot be accepted for travel. Gel/Dry cell battery operated wheelchairs are permitted to be carried in the aircraft hold provided the battery is disconnected and the battery terminals are insulated by the user before the unit is handed over to loading personnel. Passenger Wheelchairs are not permitted to travel in the cabin.

## 8.2.2.1.8 Assistance In-flight

Water is available for purchase from the bar if required for the administration of oral medication. Cabin Crew may alternatively provide passengers with potable water once it has been boiled and allowed to cool. Cabin Crew are not allowed to provide medical services such as administering injections. For safety reasons Cabin Crew cannot lift passengers or assist with personal hygiene. Passengers requiring these types of assistance must travel with an accompanying passenger.

Issue: 22, Revision: 0, Date: 01/01/26

RYANAIR

PART A – OPERATIONS MANUAL

## 8.2.2.1.9 Essential Medical Equipment/Supplies

Passengers must bring the prescription with them to allow security clearance for the carriage of medication in hand luggage. Passengers who need to carry essential medical equipment/supplies in addition to their hand or checked baggage allowance are, subject to cabin safety restrictions, approved to carry these equipment/supplies.

## 8.2.2.1.10 Denying Travel

The following are considered grounds for denying a passenger travel:

1. If the attending physician recommends that the passenger not travel (or expresses doubt in this regard).
2. If the passenger suffers from any contagious/communicable disease.
3. If the passenger's condition is such that the health/safety/welfare of Ryanair staff or other persons may be at risk.
4. If the passenger's condition is such that it may cause distress or discomfort to other passengers.
5. If the special arrangements or level of assistance required would put an undue burden on Ryanair or its agents thereby degrading the level of service to other passengers.
6. If a stretcher is required on board.

## 8.2.2.1.11 Pregnancy

Ryanair reserves the right to refuse travel if a passenger over 28 weeks pregnant does not present a ‘fit to fly’ letter from their midwife/doctor.

## 8.2.2.1.12 Transportation of Inadmissible Passengers, Deportees or Persons in Custody CAT.OP.MPA.155

## 8.2.2.1.12.1 Definitions

### Deporte

A person who is legally admitted to a State by its authorities or who enters a State illegally, and who at some time later, is formally ordered by the authorities to leave that State.

### Inadmissible Person

Persons whose entry to a State is refused by the competent authorities and who are being transported back to their country of departure, or to any other country where the person is admissible.

Issue: 22, Revision: 0, Date: 01/01/26

RYANAIR

PART A – OPERATIONS MANUAL

Person in Lawful Custody (Escorted Prisoner)

Persons either under arrest or convicted by a court of law who have to be transported.

## 8.2.2.1.12.2 Policy

The decision to accept a deportee/inadmissible person/person in lawful custody on a flight ultimately rests with the Captain. If he/she considers the carriage of such persons might jeopardise the safety/security of the passengers, crew or aircraft, carriage will be refused.

Where an escort is required, a maximum of one deportee/inadmissible person/person in lawful custody is permitted for carriage on a flight.

Persons in lawful custody shall always be escorted.

## 8.2.2.1.12.3 Notification Requirements

The competent authority shall notify Ryanair in writing in advance (where possible 24 hours in advance) when they plan to embark a deportee, inadmissible person or person in lawful custody.

The written notification shall normally be in English and shall contain the following details:

1. Identity and gender of the person;
2. Reason for transportation;
3. Name and title of escort(s) (if provided);
4. Risk assessment by the competent authority including reasons to escort or not and details of any known medical condition(s);

**Note:** If the risk assessment indicates a ‘high’ level of risk an alternative means of transport should be used.

5. Prior seating arrangements (if required);
6. Nature of available travel documents.

Ryanair will make this information available to the Captain of the flight prior to passengers boarding the aircraft.

The procedures for refusal of carriage of potentially disruptive passengers are contained in the ground operations manual.

page 8–156

Issue: 22, Revision: 0, Date: 01/01/26

RYANAIR

PART A – OPERATIONS MANUAL

## 8.2.2.1.12.4 Escorts

Persons in lawful custody must always be escorted on a flight.

For deportees and inadmissible persons, the competent authority will, on the basis of their risk assessment, decide whether or not an escort is required.

If an escort is deemed necessary, a minimum of two escorts should be provided with additional escorts as necessary.

If an escort is deemed not necessary, the deportees or inadmissible person will be escorted to the aircraft.

The escort will not leave the aircraft until the doors are ready to be closed.

## 8.2.2.1.12.5 Guidance to Commanders

1. Limitations on the carriage of Inadmissible Persons or Deportees who do NOT require an escort;

Inadmissible Persons are considered Special Category of Passengers and are therefore limited to 50% of the available seats on board the aircraft, however, the number would normally be less than 2. If presented with more than 2 Inadmissible Persons or Deportees, contact the duty pilot.

2. Documentation to be carried on board;

The Commander must be notified of INAD passenger carriage approvals. In all cases, the Ryanair Group Immigration Support Team will notify the Commander prior dispatch via appropriate channels. This will take the form of an email from immigrationsupport@ryanair.com which will contain details of the passenger and a risk assessment carried out by the Ryanair Immigration team.

All INAD pax must be included in the flight manifest.

In some states the Commander may be giving an envelope containing the passengers travel documents. This is not a requirement, however if you are presented with documentation, the documentation shall be retained on the flight deck.

If a passenger has been allowed to board in possession of their travel documentation crew shall not attempt to take it from them.

3. Boarding and during the flight;

- The Inadmissible Persons or Deportees, when escorted, are pre-boarded and will disembark last.
- The Inadmissible Persons or Deportees and escort(s) if applicable are seated at the rear of the Aircraft.

- Where possible handcuffs and other restraints are removed while onboard the aircraft.
- The Inadmissible Persons or Deportees are not permitted to occupy emergency exit seats or aisle seats.
- The Inadmissible Persons or Deportees shall have no access to alcohol.
- The Inadmissible Persons or Deportees hold baggage is loaded last to avoid delays in the event that they do not travel.

4. Disembarking at destination;

On arrival at the destination the passenger may or may not be meet by immigration.

If no immigration officer is present at the stand, then request that the handling agent inform the immigration team of the passenger and ask if they intend to attend.

Once all other passengers have disembarked, and in the absence of instruction to the contrary allow the passenger to disembark and give any travel documentation to the handling agent. Do NOT give the documentation to the passenger.

8.2.2.2 Permissible Size and Weight of Hand Baggage (Cabin Baggage) (CAT.OP.MPA.160)

The cabin baggage specifications on the Ryanair website and Ryanair GOPS procedures in force assure that only hand baggage that can be adequately and securely stowed is taken into the passenger compartment. Passengers and crew are protected against items in overhead bins moving by the design of the bins and passenger announcements advising caution that such items may have moved. Cabin baggage placed underneath seats cannot move to obstruct aisles and exits under normal flight conditions.

8.2.2.3 Securing of Passenger Compartment and Galley(s) CAT.OP.MPA.230

Before taxiing, take-off and landing all exits and escape paths will be unobstructed by complying with the procedures presented in OMA-SEP.

8.2.2.4 Positioning of Ground Equipment

All ground equipment for the servicing of the aircraft whilst on a turnaround will be positioned on the starboard side of the aircraft. This is to ensure that passengers do not come in contact with any equipment while embarking or disembarking the aircraft. The ground equipment manual describes the position of ground equipment in the vicinity of the aircraft, including the 2 m rule, OMA 8.2.1.2.

## 8.2.2.5 Operation of Aircraft Doors CAT.OP.MPA.220

Assisting means for emergency evacuation Ryanair has established procedures in OMA-SEP which ensure that before taxiing, take-off and landing and when safe and practicable to do so, all means of assistance for emergency evacuation that deploy automatically are armed.

Instructions for the operation of aircraft doors is contained in the FCOM and OMA-SEP.

Only personnel who have been given specific, hands-on training in the opening and closing of the L1 door while standing on the integral airstairs are permitted to do so. Operating L1 door from an airbridge positioned at the same level as the forward entry may be carried out by other authorised staff if unattended by flight or Cabin Crew.

## 8.2.2.5.1 Aircraft Operations in High Winds on the Ground

The chances of personal injury, aircraft or ground equipment damage increase as surface wind increases. Up to 20 kt the risk is relatively low. As the wind increases above 20 kt the risk increases exponentially.

All destination airport authorities and handling agents operate to the limits specified in this section unless they have demonstrated to Ryanair that their mobile steps are certified for use in higher windspeeds. The Ryanair acceptance of this is controlled by GOPS. If the handling agent in a destination presents steps in wind conditions in excess of 20 kt the operating crew can assume that the use of this equipment has been accepted by GOPS.

### Wind Between 20 kt and 40 kt

Confirm all ground/ramp equipment has been secured (i.e. parking brakes set or chocked). Do not use mobile steps unless the handling agency has equipment accepted by GOPS for use in excess of 20 kt windspeed – see above.

The maximum windspeed for continuance of operations on the ground, including gusts, is an absolute value of 60 kts.

Windspeed is calculated as the sum of the steady wind plus $\frac{1}{2}$ the gust, e.g. wind 240/30 gusting 50 gives a total wind speed of $30\mathrm{kt} + 10\mathrm{kt}$ s ($\frac{1}{2}$ the gust of $20\mathrm{kt}$); total wind = 40 kt.

### Operations Between 41 kt and 60 kt

If winds are forecast to exceed 40 kts and aircraft are expected to be left unattended flight crew shall contact Maintrol for guidance on the protection of aircraft on the ground.

All unnecessary ramp equipment will be removed to a sheltered location.

Issue: 22, Revision: 0, Date: 01/01/26

PART A – OPERATIONS MANUAL

The Stabilizer shall be set to Zero units of stab trim ‘Airplane full Nose Down’.

The Parking Brake will be SET and wheels double chocked.

The Aircraft will be fueled to at least 7000 kg.

Aircraft must be parked tactically\* to ensure door and air stairs are not operated when directly exposed to the wind (ref: Maintenance Manual).

Operation of selected doors with winds greater than 40 kt may be approved by the Commander where positioning of the aircraft relative to prevailing wind or proximity to local buildings or the terminal affords obvious protection.

The DO NOT DE-ICE placard shall be placed in the captains DV window. This procedure shall be applied even if there is no expectation that de-icing will be required.

![img-12.jpeg](img-12.jpeg)

Before de-icing or pre-flight return the aircraft to normal configuration for take-off. The DO NOT DE-ICE placard shall be stowed in the glare shield holder.

Note: If the aircraft has been de-iced with the stabilizer trim in the zero trim position Maintrol shall be contacted for guidance before dispatch.

## Wind at or above 60 kt

There is an absolute value of 60 kts (including gusts) at which all operations on the ground shall be suspended, and shall remain suspended until winds have abated to below 60 kts for a period of more than 30 minutes, e.g. wind 240/30 gusting 60 – all operations on the ground shall be suspended.

## 8.2.2.7 Start-up, Ramp Departure and Arrival Procedures

These procedures are contained in FCOM and the GOPS Manual. Ground Handling Agents can access procedures, instructions and guidance from the GOPS Manual.

## Use of Anti-Collision Light

Switching on the anti-collision light is a universal signal in the industry that appropriate precautions to remain clear should be taken by personnel in the vicinity of an aircraft. Illumination of the anti-collision light is an indication that a hazard exists, engines are running (or about to start), the aircraft is being towed, or that the aircraft is moving under its own thrust.

Equally, switching off the anti-collision light is an indication that the engines no longer present a hazard on the aircraft.

**Note:** Use of the anti-collision light other than for designed, approved purposes is not permitted.

**CAUTION:** If the ground power trips out and there is no other source of AC power selected, the anti-collision light does not function, though it is switched on. The number one engine may still be running and GOPS personnel could assume it is safe to approach the aircraft as they would believe the anti-collision light was switched off.

## Push back and Towing – Aeroplanes

AMC1 CAT.OP.MPA.205

## Barless Towing

Barless towing is based on the applicable SAE ARP (Aerospace Recommended Practices). Pre- or post-taxi positioning of the aeroplanes is only executed by barless towing when the aeroplane is protected by its own design from damage to the nose wheel steering system. Ryanair aircraft are protected by the bypass pin isolating the hydraulic system to the nosewheel steering and the crew procedure in FCOM Vol 1 NP.

On aircraft equipped with serviceable flight deck-to-ground intercom, this equipment will be used whenever the aircraft is on stand, and until clearance for hand signals has been given prior to departure.

All start-up, ramp departure and arrival procedures are applied as per aircraft type Standard Operating Procedures as laid down in the FCOM Vol I.

Whenever the use of hand signals is required, ground marshallers and Flight Crew Members shall use hand signals defined in ICAO Rules of the Air, Annex 2 (refer to OMA Chapter 12).

## 8.2.2.7.1 Start-up

After start-up clearance has been received from ATC, engines may be started only when an “all clear” signal or communication on the aircraft interphone system has been received from the Engineer/Mechanic or Handling Agent.

Refer to OMA 12.6 for Standard Marshalling signals used during ground operations.

When interphone capability is not available for the pushback or start procedure:

- To indicate the ground crew’s readiness for push/start, the marshaller stands on the designated marshalling position (Ground Operations Manual refers), approximately 12 metres in front of the aircraft, or as per local apron design.
- Do not turn on the anti-collision light until the marshaller is on the designated marshalling position indicating that both handling pre-departure checks are complete and the ground crew is ready for start.

Start procedures and supplementary start procedures are contained in FCOM Vol 1, Normal and Supplementary procedures.

The PM shall monitor the engine start on instruments.

During engine start sequence the Captain shall verify that the marshaller is not indicating a start incident (e.g., cut engine(s), fire) using hand signals. The Captain shall immediately respond to hand signals from the marshaller.

## 8.2.2.7.2 Push-Back Procedures

### General

Increasingly, aircraft are parked nose-in at airport terminal gates and have to be pushed back for departure.

The following are Ryanair procedures agreed with the Engineering Department. However, the general principles are good for, and should be applied at, other airports where ground personnel are provided by other organisations.

Although the Commander retains full command of the aircraft, it must be understood that the ground Crew Chief is in charge of the push-back manoeuvre and that the responsibility for the safety of the aircraft, the tug and any other person or objects in the immediate vicinity rests with him.

## 8.2.2.7.3 Ground/Flight Deck Communications

Although, in the following procedure, visual alternative signals are given for use when the ground intercom cannot be used, it must always be used if it is available or can be made available.

When using the ground intercom, it is essential that the words and phrases in FCOM Vol 1, Normal procedures are used exactly as given, since they have been chosen with care and are intended to avoid confusion. Communication from the operating crew is generally handled by the Commander. When calling, ground crew will be addressed as “GROUND” and the operating crew as “FLIGHT DECK”.

## 8.2.2.7.4 Interruption of Push-Back

Where the cockpit crew require the push-back to be stopped, the request is made on the service interphone, which is normally connected throughout the push-back.

If the tow bar becomes disconnected from the aircraft during the push-back, this will be immediately advised to the cockpit crew who will straight away apply the brakes with caution.

Issue: 22, Revision: 0, Date: 01/01/26

RYANAIR

PART A – OPERATIONS MANUAL

## 8.2.2.7.5 Re-establishment of Cockpit/Ground Communications

The cockpit crew will flash the aircraft landing lights to attract the ground personnel attention.

Particular care needs to be taken where it is necessary for ground crew to come back to the aircraft and re-establish headset communications after the engines have been started. The aircraft must remain stationary until the matter has been fully resolved and safety ensured.

The aircraft shall not taxi until a positive “all clear” or “thumbs-up” signal has been given to the Captain.

If the ground crew indicates a requirement to reconnect the headset and communicate with the cockpit, the aircraft will stop and remain stationary until the matter is fully resolved.

It is expected that a clear “thumbs-down” signal will be given, that the headset will be waved to the cockpit and that the aircraft will be approached from the front and in full view of the cockpit. However, the absence of any of these signals or indications must not in any circumstances be taken as clearance to proceed.

## 8.2.2.7.6 Power-Back, Reverse-Back Procedures

Reversing using Reverse thrust on Boeing 737 aircraft is prohibited for all Ryanair operations.

Where it is not possible to use forward thrust to manoeuvre out of a particular situation then the Commander shall first request the assistance of an aircraft tow-truck.

The Aircraft Marshaller/Ground Engineer must have two-way voice communication with the Commander, or hand signals may be used.

In addition, there must be one other competent person positioned who can confirm to the Marshaller that the area into which the aircraft is being manoeuvred is clear.

## 8.2.2.7.7 Pushback and Delayed Start Procedures

Pushbacks with delayed starts are used to free up stands and ground crew for other Ryanair aircraft to ensure punctuality. Expect to conduct push and delayed start operations at any Ryanair destination or base.

You may be towed to the hold position or you may taxi there.

PART A – OPERATIONS MANUAL

If taxing:

- Follow normal pushback and before taxi procedures;
- Start APU approaching the hold position;
- At the hold position, shut down the engines and complete the normal shut-down procedure;
- Start engines following normal engine start procedures to comply with your CTOT and depart as normal.

Note: Marshalling is not required.

Note 1: The time of the original push to hold is not block time and will be noted in the comments section of the Electronic Flight Log/Paper Voyage report. This is material information for Ground Operations statistics. After push and hold is completed, the time of start up for the purpose of take-off is the legal off block time for Company and pilot records.

Note 2: This procedure may be used during aircraft circuit training details to accomplish tire checks. In this instance, taxi the aircraft to the ATC designated hold position and:

- Start APU;
- At the hold position, shut down the engines and complete the normal shut down procedure;
- Complete the tyre check;
- Start engines following normal engine start procedures and depart as normal.

Note: Marshalling is not required.

Ryanair Captains are qualified to perform this task and only one pilot is required. Normal pre and post flight procedures apply. ## 8.2.2.8 Servicing of Aircraft

It is essential that ground clearance is received before initiating any action or operating any system which might be hazardous to personnel or equipment on the ground.

When crew approach an aircraft for the first time it is recommended that they make a mental note of work in progress around the aircraft, and establish from the Technical Log if any servicing or rectification of systems is in progress or due to take place before departure. FCOM Volume 1 specifies that the Technical Log shall be checked as the second item in the cockpit safety inspection.

### Potable Water Supply

Ryanair ensures that the uplift of potable water occurs only at stations where water is bacteriologically and chemically safe and satisfactory in taste, and where water quality meets the specifications of the appropriate health authorities.

Ground equipment used in aircraft servicing shall be operated as described in the GOPS Manual and SEP Manual.

### Potable Water – Minimum for Dispatch

Potable water is not mandatory for flight, is primarily a service issue and the following guidelines apply:

- Safety of operations takes precedence over commercial considerations.
- When established that potable water is empty and bars have NOT been loaded the aircraft will not normally depart until one of these is replenished.
- When it is established that potable water is 1/8 full and bars have NOT been loaded it is permissible to depart. On board service shall be managed to conserve remaining potable water for unexpected SEP issues.
- When potable water indication is &gt; 1/8 full and bars ARE on the aircraft normal service resumes until potable water content reduces to 1/8 full and the restrictions above apply.
- 1/8 full is between E and ¼ mark.

Sensible application of these guidelines will ensure that our passengers travel safely and punctually with minimum disruption.

- The Load and Balance Document (LID or Loadsheet)

## Mass and Balance Data and Documentation CAT.POL.MAB.105

In Ryanair when weight is used it has the same meaning as mass in this section. If a nonstandard method is used to prepare the mass and balance documentation the commander shall be informed AMC1 CAT.POL.MAB.100 a.

Using standard masses (CAT.POL.MAB.100. d.), Ryanair has established mass and balance data and produces mass and balance documentation prior to each flight specifying the load and its distribution. The mass and balance document is the Load Information Document (LID) or the paper loadsheet which is prepared according to the procedures and instructions contained in the OMB – Mass and Balance. This Manual is prepared and maintained by the Head of Flight Operation Performance Engineering. The OPT Weight and Balance App/paper loadsheet enables the commander to determine that the load and its distribution is such that the mass and balance limits of the aircraft are not exceeded.

A completed paper Loadsheet contains the following information:

- aircraft registration and type;
- flight identification, number and date;
- name of the commander;
- name of the person who prepared the document;
- dry operating mass and the corresponding CG of the aircraft;
- mass of the fuel at take-off and the mass of trip fuel;
- load components including passengers, baggage, freight and ballast;
- take-off mass, landing mass and zero fuel mass;
- applicable aircraft CG positions; and
- the limiting mass and CG values.

The person supervising the loading of the aircraft confirms by signature or equivalent on the Load Information Document (LID)/paper loadsheet presented to the commander that the load and its distribution is compliant with the LCM. The commander indicates acceptance by writing his crewcode legibly and his signature on the paper LID/paper Loadsheet. The commander shall always ask the dispatcher to “confirm the status of the rear hold”.

The person supervising the loading of the aircraft confirms that the load and its distribution are in accordance with the eLID by entering their PIN code in the GOPs App. The Commander signifies acceptance of the eLID by entering their PIN code in the EFL App.

Ryanair has specified procedures for last minute changes (CAT.POL.MAB.105.d.) to the paper Loadsheet to ensure that:

1. any last minute change after the completion of the paper Loadsheet is brought to the attention of the commander and entered in the paper Loadsheet;
2. the maximum last minute change allowed in passenger numbers or hold load is specified as 500 kgs or less;
3. new mass and balance documentation is prepared if LMC of 501 kg or more (paper Loadsheet only).

# NOTOC

NOTOC is an ICAO term, Notice to Captain. In Ryanair it indicates to the Captain that dangerous goods are being carried.

# Shippers Declaration for Dangerous Goods

This documentation is prepared by the organisation shipping the DG. In Ryanair this is the Engineering Department moving spare parts and equipment related to aircraft servicing within the Ryanair network.

# Consignment Note

This is prepared by the agency in an airport which processes the DG from the shipper to the aircraft.
